Compare saltwater concentration and corrosion references

CL-0107 · Class 7 · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited

Aim and prediction

Compare matched coupon reference scenarios while recording every controlled input.

Apparatus and principle

Two virtual maintained carrier baths and matched edge-masked iron coupons compare solution properties, oxygen supply and a selected current-ceiling fraction. Ionic resistance and oxygen transport are separate limits. Cleaned-metal loss and retained-product gain are different quantities. These are chosen reference scenarios, not measured corrosion-rate data.

Key terms and interpretation

  • Mass percent compares salt mass with the final solution mass. Molarity uses solution volume; molality uses solvent mass. They are different concentration definitions.
  • Conductivity describes how readily the carrier supports ionic charge movement. Resistance also depends on the selected path length and area.
  • A current ceiling is a conditional limit within the stated model. The selected fraction sets a reference scenario; it is not a measured corrosion efficiency.
  • The Faraday constant is charge per mole of electron equivalents. The primary FeII account uses two per converted Fe; further FeIII maturation is tracked separately.
  • Cleaned-metal loss counts iron converted. A retained specimen can gain mass because oxygen and hydration water enter its product account.
  • An open material balance includes external material supplied. A maintained bath must not be analysed as though it were a sealed container.

Procedure and observation instructions

  1. Write the aim and prediction. Compare salt concentration while matching initial iron mass, prepared exposed two-face area and carrier-solution mass. State whether oxygen and other reference settings are also matched.
  2. Choose dilute or entered-property mode. Dilute mode uses a restricted leading-order conductivity approximation and selected density. Broader concentrations require supplied conductivity and density. Entered reference values are not automatically verified measurements.
  3. Record all inputs before advancing time. Calculate salt mass from percent of the final solution mass, then water mass by difference. Do not use grams per 100 g of water as though it were final-solution mass percent.
  4. Record the initial matched observation. The model uses ideal maintained baths: carrier composition and bulk oxygen are held constant by replenishment. This is not a sealed test-tube mass account.
  5. Advance the same illustrative model time for both coupons and record both cells together. Distinguish resistance and oxygen ceilings from the selected fraction of that ceiling. A changed oxygen input is an additional variable, so the outcome cannot be attributed to salt alone.
  6. Record primary charge, cleaned-metal loss, retained-product gain and externally supplied oxygen/water. The extra oxygen for assumed FeII-to-FeIII maturation is included separately from primary cathodic reduction.
  7. Repeat with equal solutions, zero driving drop, zero selected fraction or an oxygen-limited comparison. At metal exhaustion, current and further charge accumulation stop; the time average can remain positive.
  8. Show formula, substituted values, result and units. Write a conclusion limited to the chosen reference assumptions. A physical cleaned-metal-loss experiment needs a defined cleaning/drying procedure and blank correction; weighing a wet or rust-coated specimen does not directly give cleaned-metal loss.

At 7 illustrative model days, cleaned-metal loss is 0.0018022727731 g in A and 0.0180144891056 g in B. Retained-specimen gain is 0.00106519500669 g in A and 0.0106470808026 g in B. The B−A cleaned-loss difference is 0.0162122163325 g. Interpret the comparison using all recorded settings, including oxygen and the chosen current-ceiling fraction; it is not an experimentally measured salt-only rate law.

Result and interpretation

Use the paired observations and all controlled inputs to interpret the conditional comparison. More salt alone does not establish a universal measured rust-rate ranking. A different oxygen input or entered conductivity changes the scenario. Current ceases after metal exhaustion while the time-averaged current can remain positive.

Student conclusion

These are calculated reference results; distinguish cleaned loss, retained gain and externally supplied material.

Assessment criteria

  • Correct final-solution mass-percent preparation and explicit units.
  • Clear distinction between controlled and changed variables.
  • Correct cleaned-metal-loss, retained-gain and open-account interpretation.
  • Correct primary versus total-redox oxygen/charge accounting and appropriately limited conclusion.

Precautions and model limitations

  • This is a conditional transport/current-ceiling reference, not an empirically calibrated corrosion-rate law. The selected effective electrolyte drop is not a complete applied cell voltage or an electrode-kinetics model. Activation polarization, oxide barriers, pH changes and chloride-specific surface reactions are omitted.
  • Dilute mode supports 0–0.05 mass-percent NaCl with selected limiting molar conductivity 0.01265 S m²/mol, water background 5.5e-6 S/m and ideal density 1 g/mL. Finite-concentration interactions are neglected. Entered mode supports broader compositions using supplied properties; empirical consistency of those inputs is not certified.
  • Bulk oxygen is an independently supplied boundary value. The selected diffusivity 2e-9 m²/s and boundary-layer geometry define a reference transport ceiling; dissolved oxygen is not falsely inferred from salt percentage or a seawater-salinity table.
  • Primary FeII charge corresponds to two electron equivalents per converted Fe. Further maturation contributes an additional redox equivalent and oxygen demand. Total redox-equivalent charge is not the same as measured primary circuit charge.
  • The selected mature product is an Fe₂O₃·H₂O accounting equivalent, not a unique composition of all real rust. All reference product is retained; flaking and dissolved-intermediate inventories are omitted. No rust density or layer thickness is predicted.
  • The analysed open boundary includes carrier, iron and the net oxygen/hydration water supplied. Constant trace dissolved-oxygen inventory, apparatus and unchanged inert gases are excluded from the incremental account. Carrier composition is maintained by ideal replenishment.
  • Only the two planar coupon faces are exposed; edges are masked. The selected nominal area also serves as the effective transport/conduction area. Equivalent cleaned-metal thickness is a uniform-loss representation, not a prediction of local pits. Prepared bath levels and qualitative surface art are not measured rust-volume data.
  • Displayed digits are computational, not measured precision. Tiny conservation residuals are floating-point diagnostics. Mass differences near exhaustion have stock-scale numerical uncertainty. The report records simulated reference results and student writing, not a certificate of physical laboratory observations.
